5.1.1
) respectively.

5.2.
The second approximate solution via first correction

5.2.1.

In this part, we want to construct a correction function to eliminate the terms
\epsilon S_{1}
and
\epsilon S_{2}

in the error
S(w_{0})
.
By the same method in
[
21
]
, we first choose correction terms
\phi_{11},{\phi_{12}}
to cancel the terms

S_{1},S_{2}.

That is to say, for fixed
\tau\in[0,2\pi)
,
we need solutions of

\displaystyle\phi_{11,xx}-\phi_{11}+3U^{2}\phi_{11}\,=\,-S_{1},\quad\phi_{11}(\pm\infty,\tau)\,=\,0,

\displaystyle\phi_{12,xx}-{\phi_{12}}+3U^{2}{\phi_{12}}\,=\,-S_{2},\quad{\phi_{12}}(\pm\infty,\tau)\,=\,0.

As it is well known, those problems are solvable provided that

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}S_{1}\,U^{\prime}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,=\,0,\qquad\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}S_{2}\,U^{\prime}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,=\,0.

Using the identities (
3.34
),
we can get

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}S_{1}\,U^{\prime}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,=\,

\displaystyle\frac{\kappa}{\beta}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\Big[U^{\prime}+\frac{2}{3}xU\Big]\,U^{\prime}\,{\mathrm{d}}x=0.

Thus, for
\phi_{11}
, we have

\displaystyle\phi_{11}(x,\tau)\,=\,\frac{\kappa}{\beta}\varpi_{1}(x),

and
\varpi_{1}
is the unique solution of

\displaystyle\varpi_{1,xx}-\varpi_{1}+3U^{2}\varpi_{1}\,=\,-\Big(U^{\prime}+\frac{2}{3}xU\Big),\quad\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\varpi_{1}U^{\prime}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,=\,0.

(5.16)

In fact, the function
\varpi_{1}
is an odd function and enjoys the exponential decay

|\varpi_{1}|\leq C|x|e^{-c|x|},\quad\text{when $|x|$ is large}.

On the other hand, we get

\displaystyle{\phi_{12}}(x,\tau)\,=\,\frac{2}{3}\frac{\kappa}{\,\beta\,}f\varpi_{2}(x),

with

\varpi_{2}(x)\,=\,-\frac{1}{2}(U+xU^{\prime}),

and
\varpi_{2}
satisfies

\displaystyle\varpi_{2,xx}-\varpi_{2}+3U^{2}\varpi_{2}\,=\,-U.

(5.17)

Therefore, we define the correction as

\displaystyle\epsilon\phi_{1}(x,\tau)\,=\,\epsilon{\phi_{11}}(x,\tau)+\epsilon{\phi_{12}}(x,\tau)=\epsilon{\mathbf{b}}_{1}(\epsilon z)\varpi_{1}(x)\ +\ \epsilon\frac{2}{3}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}(\epsilon z)f(\tau)\varpi_{2}(x),

(5.18)

where the relation between
z
and
\tau
is given in (
3.20
)
and

{\mathbf{b}}_{1}(\theta)=\frac{\kappa(\theta)}{\beta(\theta)}.

(5.19)

Then we take

\displaystyle w_{1}(x,\tau)\,=\,w_{0}(x,\tau)\,+\,\epsilon\phi_{1}(x,\tau)

(5.20)

as the
second approximate solution
.
From (
5.15
), we can derive that

\displaystyle S(w_{0}+\epsilon\phi_{1})\,=\,

\displaystyle S(w_{0})+\epsilon\Big[\phi_{1,xx}-\phi_{1}+3U^{2}\phi_{1}\Big]+\frac{\epsilon^{2}4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\epsilon\phi_{1,\tau\tau}

\displaystyle+\left[\left(w_{0}+\epsilon\phi_{1}\right)^{3}-w_{0}^{3}-\epsilon 3U^{2}\phi_{1}\right]+B_{4}(\epsilon\phi_{1})+B_{5}(\epsilon\phi_{1})+B_{6}(\epsilon\phi_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{2}S_{3}+\epsilon^{2}S_{4}+\epsilon^{3}S_{5}+\epsilon^{3}S_{6}+\epsilon^{3}S_{7}+\epsilon^{3}S_{8}+\frac{1}{\beta}\epsilon^{3}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}e^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{Z}+\frac{1}{\beta}\epsilon\lambda_{0}e{\mathcal{Z}}

\displaystyle\underbrace{+\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{\kappa a_{2}}{\,\beta^{2}\,}e^{\prime\prime}(x+f)\mathcal{Z}-\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{\kappa a_{2}}{\beta^{2}}(x+f)f^{\prime\prime}e\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}\right]}_{\text{terms involving}~f^{\prime\prime},e^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{G}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}},e,\frac{e^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon^{2}4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\epsilon\phi_{1,\tau\tau}+\mathbb{N}_{0}(\epsilon\phi_{1})+B_{4}(\epsilon\phi_{1})+B_{5}(\epsilon\phi_{1})+B_{6}(\epsilon\phi_{1}),

(5.21)

where the linear operators
B_{4},B_{5},B_{6}
are given in (
3.26
), (
3.2
), (
3.28
),
and the nonlinear term is

\displaystyle\mathbb{N}_{0}(\epsilon\phi_{1})=\left(w_{0}+\epsilon\phi_{1}\right)^{3}-w_{0}^{3}-\epsilon 3U^{2}\phi_{1}.

(5.22)

By some calculations, we provide the following facts

\displaystyle\phi_{1,x}={\mathbf{b}}_{1}\varpi_{1,x}+\frac{2}{3}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}f\varpi_{2,x},\qquad\phi_{1,xx}={\mathbf{b}}_{1}\varpi_{1,xx}+\frac{2}{3}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}f\varpi_{2,xx},

\displaystyle\phi_{1,\tau}=

\displaystyle{\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{\prime}\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}}{2\pi\beta}\varpi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{\prime}\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}}{2\pi\beta}f\varpi_{2}+\frac{2}{3}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}f^{\prime}\varpi_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}}{2\pi}{\mathbf{b}}_{2}\varpi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}}{2\pi}{\mathbf{b}}_{2}f\varpi_{2}+\frac{2}{3}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}f^{\prime}\varpi_{2},

where

\displaystyle{\mathbf{b}}_{2}(\theta)=\frac{{\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{\prime}(\theta)}{\beta(\theta)},

and

\displaystyle\phi_{1,x\tau}=

\displaystyle\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}}{2\pi}{\mathbf{b}}_{2}\varpi_{1,x}+\frac{2}{3}\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}}{2\pi}{\mathbf{b}}_{2}f\varpi_{2,x}+\frac{2}{3}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}f^{\prime}\varpi_{2,x},

\displaystyle\phi_{1,\tau\tau}=

\displaystyle\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}}{2\pi}{\mathbf{b}}_{2}^{\prime}\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}}{2\pi\beta}\varpi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}}{2\pi}{\mathbf{b}}_{2}^{\prime}\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}}{2\pi\beta}f\varpi_{2}+\frac{2}{3}\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}}{2\pi}{\mathbf{b}}_{2}f^{\prime}\varpi_{2}+\frac{2}{3}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{\prime}\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}}{2\pi\beta}f^{\prime}\varpi_{2}+\frac{2}{3}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}f^{\prime\prime}\varpi_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}{\mathbf{b}}_{3}\varpi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}{\mathbf{b}}_{3}f\varpi_{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}}{2\pi}{\mathbf{b}}_{2}f^{\prime}\varpi_{2}+\frac{2}{3}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}f^{\prime\prime}\varpi_{2},

where

\displaystyle{\mathbf{b}}_{3}(\theta)=\frac{{\mathbf{b}}_{2}^{\prime}(\theta)}{\beta(\theta)}.

(5.23)

5.2.2.
More precise expression of
S(w_{1})

We will do the following analysis.

\clubsuit

From the expression of
\epsilon^{2}S_{4}
as in (
5.1.2
), we can further decompose the term
\epsilon^{2}S_{4}
as follows

\displaystyle\epsilon^{2}S_{4}=\epsilon^{2}S_{41}+\epsilon^{2}S_{42},

(5.24)

where

\displaystyle\epsilon^{2}S_{41}

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{2}\Bigg\{\frac{\beta^{\prime\prime}}{\beta^{3}}U-\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{\epsilon^{2}W_{tt}(0,\epsilon z)}{\beta^{4}}x^{2}U+\Big[\frac{2|\beta^{\prime}|^{2}}{\beta^{4}}+\frac{\beta^{\prime\prime}}{\beta^{3}}\,-\,\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\Big]xU^{\prime}+\frac{|\beta^{\prime}|^{2}}{\beta^{4}}x^{2}U^{\prime\prime}\Bigg\},

(5.25)

\displaystyle\epsilon^{2}S_{42}

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{2}\Bigg\{-\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{\epsilon^{2}W_{tt}(0,\epsilon z)}{\beta^{4}}|f|^{2}U+\Big[\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}|f^{\prime}|^{2}-\frac{4\pi}{{\tilde{\ell}}}\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta^{2}}ff^{\prime}+\frac{|\beta^{\prime}|^{2}}{\beta^{4}}|f|^{2}\Big]U^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{2}{3}\frac{\kappa}{\,\beta^{2}\,}fe{\mathcal{Z}}+\frac{3}{\beta^{2}}e^{2}U{\mathcal{Z}}^{2}\Bigg\}.

(5.26)

\clubsuit

Moreover, by the definitions of
B_{4}
,
\epsilon\phi_{1}
as in (
3.26
), (
5.18
), we can decompose
B_{4}(\epsilon\phi_{1})
as

\displaystyle B_{4}(\epsilon\phi_{1})\,=\,

\displaystyle\epsilon^{2}\frac{\kappa}{\,\beta\,}\Big[\phi_{1,x}+\frac{2}{3}x\phi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}f\phi_{1}\Big]

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle\epsilon^{2}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{2}\Big[\varpi_{1,x}+\frac{2}{3}f\varpi_{2,x}+\frac{2}{3}x\varpi_{1}+\frac{4}{9}fx\varpi_{2}+\frac{2}{3}f\varpi_{1}+\frac{4}{9}f^{2}\varpi_{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle\epsilon^{2}{\bf B}_{41}+\epsilon^{2}{\bf B}_{42},

(5.27)

with

\displaystyle{\bf B}_{41}={\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{2}\Big[\varpi_{1,x}+\frac{2}{3}x\varpi_{1}\Big],\qquad{\bf B}_{42}={\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{2}\Big[\frac{2}{3}f\varpi_{2,x}+\frac{4}{9}fx\varpi_{2}+\frac{2}{3}f\varpi_{1}+\frac{4}{9}f^{2}\varpi_{2}\Big].

(5.28)

\clubsuit

From the expression of
B_{5}
given in (
3.2
), we have

\displaystyle B_{5}(\epsilon\phi_{1})\,=\,

\displaystyle\epsilon^{3}\Bigg\{\frac{2\pi}{{\tilde{\ell}}}\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta^{2}}\Big[-f^{\prime}\phi_{1,x}+\phi_{1,\tau}\Big]+\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\Big[|f^{\prime}|^{2}\phi_{1,xx}-f^{\prime\prime}\phi_{1,x}-2f^{\prime}\phi_{1,x\tau}\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{4\pi}{{\tilde{\ell}}}\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta^{2}}(x+f)\big[-f^{\prime}\phi_{1,xx}+\phi_{1,x\tau}\big]+\Big[\frac{\beta^{\prime\prime}}{\beta^{3}}\,-\,\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\Big](x+f)\phi_{1,x}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{|\beta^{\prime}|^{2}}{\beta^{4}}(x+f)^{2}\phi_{1,xx}-\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{\epsilon^{2}W_{tt}(0,\epsilon z)}{\beta^{4}}(x+f)^{2}\phi_{1}\Bigg\}.

(5.29)

\clubsuit

By the definition of
B_{6}
given in (
3.28
), we
select the terms from
B_{6}(\epsilon\phi_{1})
involving
f^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\epsilon^{4}\kappa(\epsilon z)a_{2}(\epsilon s,\epsilon z)\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{1}{\beta}(x+f)\big[-f^{\prime\prime}\phi_{1,x}+\phi_{1,\tau\tau}\big]

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{4}\kappa(\epsilon z)a_{2}(\epsilon s,\epsilon z)\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{1}{\beta}(x+f)\Bigg[-f^{\prime\prime}\big({\mathbf{b}}_{1}\varpi_{1,x}+\frac{2}{3}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}f\varpi_{2,x}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\Big(\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}{\mathbf{b}}_{3}\varpi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}{\mathbf{b}}_{3}f\varpi_{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}}{2\pi}{\mathbf{b}}_{2}f^{\prime}\varpi_{2}+\frac{2}{3}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}f^{\prime\prime}\varpi_{2}\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\epsilon^{4}\kappa(\epsilon z)a_{2}(\epsilon s,\epsilon z)\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{1}{\beta}(x+f)\Bigg[-f^{\prime\prime}\big({\mathbf{b}}_{1}\varpi_{1,x}+\frac{2}{3}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}f\varpi_{2,x}\big)+\frac{2}{3}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}f^{\prime\prime}\varpi_{2}\Bigg]}_{:=\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}\right]}

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon^{4}\kappa(\epsilon z)a_{2}(\epsilon s,\epsilon z)\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{1}{\beta}(x+f)\Big(\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}{\mathbf{b}}_{3}\varpi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}{\mathbf{b}}_{3}f\varpi_{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}}{2\pi}{\mathbf{b}}_{2}f^{\prime}\varpi_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon^{4}\kappa(\epsilon z)a_{2}(\epsilon s,\epsilon z)\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{1}{\beta}(x+f)\Big(\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}{\mathbf{b}}_{3}\varpi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}{\mathbf{b}}_{3}f\varpi_{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}}{2\pi}{\mathbf{b}}_{2}f^{\prime}\varpi_{2}\Big).

(5.30)

And we can rewrite
B_{6}(\epsilon\phi_{1})
as

\displaystyle B_{6}(\epsilon\phi_{1})=\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{2}\left[\theta,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}\right],

(5.31)

where

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{2}\left[\theta,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{6}\frac{\,\,\epsilon^{2}W_{ttt}(0,\epsilon z)}{\beta^{5}}(x+f)^{3}\phi_{1}-\frac{\epsilon^{5}}{24}\frac{\,\,\epsilon^{2}W_{tttt}\big(\hbar(\epsilon z)\epsilon s,\,\epsilon z\big)}{\beta^{6}}(x+f)^{4}\phi_{1}

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{4}\kappa^{3}(\epsilon z)a_{3}(\epsilon s,\epsilon z)\frac{1}{\,\beta^{3}\,}(x+f)^{2}\phi_{1,x}

\displaystyle\,+\,\epsilon^{4}\kappa(\epsilon z)a_{2}(\epsilon s,\epsilon z)\Bigg\{\frac{\beta^{\prime\prime}}{\beta^{4}}(x+f)\phi_{1}+\frac{2|\beta^{\prime}|^{2}}{\beta^{5}}(x+f)^{2}\phi_{1,x}+\frac{6\pi}{{\tilde{\ell}}}\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta^{3}}(x+f)\big[-f^{\prime}\phi_{1,x}+\phi_{1,\tau}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{4\pi}{{\tilde{\ell}}}\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta^{3}}(x+f)^{2}[-f^{\prime}w_{xx}+w_{x\tau}]+\frac{|\beta^{\prime}|^{2}}{\beta^{5}}(x+f)^{3}\phi_{1,xx}+\frac{\beta^{\prime\prime}}{\beta^{4}}(x+f)^{2}\phi_{1,x}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{1}{\beta}(x+f)\big[|f^{\prime}|^{2}\phi_{1,xx}-2f^{\prime}\phi_{1,x\tau}\big]\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\,+\,\epsilon^{4}\kappa^{\prime}(\epsilon z)a_{1}(\epsilon s,\epsilon z)\Bigg\{\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta^{4}}(x+f)\phi_{1}+\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta^{4}}(x+f)^{2}\phi_{1,x}+\frac{2\pi}{{\tilde{\ell}}}\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}(x+f)\big[-f^{\prime}\phi_{1,x}+\phi_{1,\tau}\big]\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{4}\kappa(\epsilon z)a_{2}(\epsilon s,\epsilon z)\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{1}{\beta}(x+f)\Big(\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}{\mathbf{b}}_{3}\varpi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}{\mathbf{b}}_{3}f\varpi_{2}+\frac{4}{3}\frac{{\tilde{\ell}}}{2\pi}{\mathbf{b}}_{2}f^{\prime}\varpi_{2}\Big).

(5.32)

\clubsuit

Similarly, for
\mathbb{N}_{0}(\epsilon\phi_{1})
as in (
5.22
), we also have the decomposition

\displaystyle\mathbb{N}_{0}(\epsilon\phi_{1})\,=\,

\displaystyle\left[\frac{\epsilon^{2}6eU\mathcal{Z}\phi_{1}}{\beta}+\frac{\epsilon^{3}3e^{2}\mathcal{Z}^{2}\phi_{1}}{\beta^{2}}+\epsilon^{2}3U\phi_{1}^{2}+\frac{\epsilon^{3}3e{\mathcal{Z}}\phi_{1}^{2}}{\beta}+\epsilon^{3}\phi_{1}^{3}\right]

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{2}6eU{\mathcal{Z}}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}}{\beta}\Big[\varpi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}f\varpi_{2}\Big]+\epsilon^{2}3{\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{2}U\Big[\varpi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}f\varpi_{2}\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon^{3}3e^{2}\mathcal{Z}^{2}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}}{\beta^{2}}\Big[\varpi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}f\varpi_{2}\Big]+\frac{\epsilon^{3}3e{\mathcal{Z}}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{2}}{\beta}\Big[\varpi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}f\varpi_{2}\Big]^{2}+\epsilon^{3}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{3}\Big[\varpi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}f\varpi_{2}\Big]^{3}

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{2}6eU{\mathcal{Z}}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}}{\beta}\Big[\varpi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}f\varpi_{2}\Big]+\epsilon^{2}3{\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{2}U\Big[\varpi_{1}^{2}+\frac{4}{3}f\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2}+\frac{4}{9}|f|^{2}\varpi_{2}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon^{3}3e^{2}\mathcal{Z}^{2}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}}{\beta^{2}}\Big[\varpi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}f\varpi_{2}\Big]+\frac{\epsilon^{3}3e{\mathcal{Z}}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{2}}{\beta}\Big[\varpi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}f\varpi_{2}\Big]^{2}+\epsilon^{3}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{3}\Big[\varpi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}f\varpi_{2}\Big]^{3}

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle\epsilon^{2}{\bf N}_{01}+\epsilon^{2}{\bf N}_{02}+\epsilon^{3}{\bf N}_{03},

(5.33)

with

\displaystyle{\bf N}_{01}=3{\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{2}U\varpi_{1}^{2},

(5.34)

\displaystyle{\bf N}_{02}=\frac{6{\mathbf{b}}_{1}eU{\mathcal{Z}}\varpi_{1}+4{\mathbf{b}}_{1}feU{\mathcal{Z}}\varpi_{2}}{\beta}+\frac{4}{3}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{2}|f|^{2}U\varpi_{2}^{2}+4{\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{2}fU\varpi_{1}\varpi_{2},

(5.35)

\displaystyle{\bf N}_{03}=

\displaystyle\frac{3e^{2}\mathcal{Z}^{2}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}}{\beta^{2}}\Big[\varpi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}f\varpi_{2}\Big]+\frac{3e{\mathcal{Z}}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{2}}{\beta}\Big[\varpi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}f\varpi_{2}\Big]^{2}+{\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{3}\Big[\varpi_{1}+\frac{2}{3}f\varpi_{2}\Big]^{3}.

(5.36)

Combining (
5.2.1
), (
5.24
), (
5.2.2
), (
5.2.2
), we can get

\displaystyle S(w_{1})\,=\,

\displaystyle\,\epsilon^{2}S_{3}+\epsilon^{2}S_{41}+\epsilon^{3}S_{42}+\epsilon^{3}S_{5}+\epsilon^{3}S_{6}+\epsilon^{3}S_{7}+\epsilon^{3}S_{8}+\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}e^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{Z}+\frac{1}{\beta}\epsilon\lambda_{0}e{\mathcal{Z}}

\displaystyle\underbrace{+\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{\kappa a_{2}}{\,\beta^{2}\,}e^{\prime\prime}(x+f)\mathcal{Z}-\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{\kappa a_{2}}{\,\beta^{2}\,}(x+f)f^{\prime\prime}e\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}\right]}_{\text{terms involving}~f^{\prime\prime},e^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\epsilon^{2}{\bf B}_{41}+\epsilon^{2}{\bf B}_{42}}_{B_{4}(\epsilon\phi_{1})}+\underbrace{\epsilon^{2}{\bf N}_{01}+\epsilon^{2}{\bf N}_{02}+\epsilon^{3}{\bf N}_{03}}_{\mathbb{N}_{0}(\epsilon\phi_{1})}+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{G}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}},e,\frac{e^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]+\epsilon^{3}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}{\phi}_{1,\tau\tau}+B_{5}(\epsilon\phi_{1})+B_{6}(\epsilon\phi_{1}).

Note that,
B_{5}(\epsilon\phi_{1}),B_{6}(\epsilon\phi_{1})
as in (
5.2.2
), (
5.31
).

5.3.
The third approximate solution via second correction

In this part, we want to eliminate the terms

\epsilon^{2}S_{41},\epsilon^{2}{\bf B}_{41},\epsilon^{2}{\bf N}_{01}

in
S(w_{1})
by finding a solution to the problem

\displaystyle\phi_{2,xx}-\phi_{2}+3U^{2}\phi_{2}\,=\,-\left[S_{41}+{\bf B}_{41}+{\bf N}_{01}\right],\quad\phi_{2}(\pm\infty,\tau)\,=\,0.

(5.37)

In order to solve (
5.37
), we need to consider the following linear problem

\displaystyle\varpi_{2,xx}-\varpi_{2}+3U^{2}\varpi_{2}\,=\,-U,\qquad\varpi_{2}(\pm\infty)\,=\,0,

\displaystyle\varpi_{3,xx}-\varpi_{3}+3U^{2}\varpi_{3}\,=\,-x^{2}U,\qquad\varpi_{3}(\pm\infty)\,=\,0,

\displaystyle\varpi_{4,xx}-\varpi_{4}+3U^{2}\varpi_{4}\,=\,-xU^{\prime},\qquad\varpi_{4}(\pm\infty)\,=\,0,

\displaystyle\varpi_{5,xx}-\varpi_{5}+3U^{2}\varpi_{5}\,=\,-x^{2}U^{\prime\prime},\qquad\varpi_{5}(\pm\infty)\,=\,0,

\displaystyle\varpi_{6,xx}-\varpi_{6}+3U^{2}\varpi_{6}\,=\,-\varpi_{1,x},\qquad\varpi_{6}(\pm\infty)\,=\,0,

\displaystyle\varpi_{7,xx}-\varpi_{7}+3U^{2}\varpi_{7}\,=\,-x\varpi_{1},\qquad\varpi_{7}(\pm\infty)\,=\,0,

\displaystyle\varpi_{8,xx}-\varpi_{8}+3U^{2}\varpi_{8}\,=\,-U\varpi_{1}^{2},\qquad\varpi_{8}(\pm\infty)\,=\,0.

Recall the expressions of
\epsilon^{2}S_{41}
,
\epsilon^{2}{\bf B}_{41}
,
\epsilon^{2}{\bf N}_{01}
given in (
5.25
), (
5.28
) and (
5.35
) respectively.
Then we can give the solution of (
5.37
) as

\displaystyle\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2}=\epsilon^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{7}\phi_{2j},

(5.38)

where

\displaystyle\phi_{21}=\frac{\beta^{\prime\prime}}{\beta^{3}}\varpi_{2},\qquad\phi_{22}=-\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{\epsilon^{2}W_{tt}(0,\epsilon z)}{\beta^{4}}\varpi_{3},\qquad\phi_{23}=\Big[\frac{2|\beta^{\prime}|^{2}}{\beta^{4}}+\frac{\beta^{\prime\prime}}{\beta^{3}}\,-\,\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\Big]\varpi_{4},

\displaystyle\phi_{24}=\frac{|\beta^{\prime}|^{2}}{\beta^{4}}\varpi_{5},\qquad\qquad\phi_{25}={\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{2}\varpi_{6},\qquad\qquad\phi_{26}=\frac{2}{3}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{2}\varpi_{7},\qquad\phi_{27}=3{\mathbf{b}}_{1}^{2}\varpi_{8}.

(5.39)

We observe that
\phi_{2}
is an even function of
x
.

As a conclusion, we take

\displaystyle w_{2}(x,\tau)\,=\,w_{1}(x,\tau)\,+\,\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2}(x,\tau)

(5.40)

as the
third approximate solution
.
Then we have the new error

\displaystyle S(w_{2})\,=\,

\displaystyle S(w_{1})+\epsilon^{2}\Big[\phi_{2,xx}-\phi_{2}+3U^{2}\phi_{2}\Big]+\left[\left(w_{1}+\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2}\right)^{3}-w_{1}^{3}-\epsilon^{2}3U^{2}\phi_{2}\right]

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\phi_{2,\tau\tau}+B_{4}(\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2})+B_{5}(\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2})+B_{6}(\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2})

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle\epsilon^{2}S_{3}+\epsilon^{2}S_{42}+\epsilon^{3}S_{5}+\epsilon^{3}S_{6}+\epsilon^{3}S_{7}+\epsilon^{3}S_{8}+\frac{1}{\beta}\epsilon^{3}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}e^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{Z}+\frac{1}{\beta}\epsilon\lambda_{0}e{\mathcal{Z}}+\epsilon^{2}{\bf N}_{02}+\epsilon^{3}{\bf N}_{03}

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{2}{\bf B}_{42}\underbrace{+\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{\kappa a_{2}}{\,\beta^{2}\,}e^{\prime\prime}(x+f)\mathcal{Z}-\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{\kappa a_{2}}{\,\beta^{2}\,}(x+f)f^{\prime\prime}e\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}\right]}_{\text{terms involving}~f^{\prime\prime},e^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{3}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}{\phi}_{1,\tau\tau}+\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\phi_{2,\tau\tau}+B_{5}(\epsilon\phi_{1})+B_{6}(\epsilon\phi_{1})

\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{G}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}},e,\frac{e^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{N}_{1}(\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2})+B_{4}(\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2})+B_{5}(\epsilon^{2}{\phi_{2}})+B_{6}(\epsilon^{2}{\phi_{2}}),

(5.41)

where the linear operators
B_{4},B_{5},B_{6}
are given in (
3.26
), (
3.2
), (
3.28
).
The term
\mathbb{N}_{1}(\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2})
is given by

\displaystyle\mathbb{N}_{1}(\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2})\,=\,

\displaystyle\left(w_{1}+\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2}\right)^{3}-w_{1}^{3}-\epsilon^{2}3U^{2}\phi_{2}

\displaystyle\,=\,

\displaystyle\epsilon^{3}\frac{6e}{\beta}U{\mathcal{Z}}\phi_{2}+\epsilon^{3}6U\phi_{1}\phi_{2}+\epsilon^{4}\frac{3e^{2}}{\beta^{2}}{\mathcal{Z}}^{2}\phi_{2}+\epsilon^{4}3\phi_{1}^{2}\phi_{2}+\epsilon^{4}\frac{6e}{\beta}{\mathcal{Z}}\phi_{1}\phi_{2}

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{4}3U\phi_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{5}\frac{3e}{\beta}{\mathcal{Z}}\phi_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{5}3\phi_{1}\phi_{2}^{2}+\epsilon^{6}\phi_{2}^{3},

(5.42)

where
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}
are given in (
5.18
) and (
5.38
).

\clubsuit

From the expression of
\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2}
as in (
5.38
), (
5.3
), and the definition of the operator
B_{4}
as in (
3.26
), it is easy to notice that the terms in
B_{4}(\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2})
are independent of
f^{\prime\prime},e^{\prime\prime}
.

\clubsuit

By the definition of
B_{5}
given in (
3.2
), we have

\displaystyle B_{5}(\epsilon^{2}{\phi_{2}})\,=\,

\displaystyle\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\Big[|f^{\prime}|^{2}\phi_{2,xx}-f^{\prime\prime}\phi_{2,x}-2f^{\prime}\phi_{2,x\tau}\Big]+\epsilon^{4}\frac{6\pi}{{\tilde{\ell}}}\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta^{2}}\Big[-f^{\prime}\phi_{2,x}+\phi_{2,\tau}\Big]

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi}{{\tilde{\ell}}}\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta^{2}}(x+f)\big[-f^{\prime}\phi_{2,xx}+\phi_{2,x\tau}\big]+\epsilon^{4}\Big[\frac{\beta^{\prime\prime}}{\beta^{3}}\,+\,\frac{2|\beta^{\prime}|^{2}}{\beta^{4}}\,-\,\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\Big](x+f)\phi_{2,x}

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{4}\frac{\beta^{\prime\prime}}{\beta^{3}}\phi_{2}+\epsilon^{4}\frac{|\beta^{\prime}|^{2}}{\beta^{4}}(x+f)^{2}\phi_{2,xx}-\frac{1}{2}\,\epsilon^{4}\frac{\epsilon^{2}W_{tt}(0,\epsilon z)}{\beta^{4}}(x+f)^{2}\phi_{2}.

(5.43)

Here we note that the less regular term is

-\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}f^{\prime\prime}\phi_{2,x}.

\clubsuit

We select the term in
B_{6}(\epsilon^{2}{\phi_{2}})
that involves
f^{\prime\prime}
, which is

\displaystyle-\epsilon^{5}\kappa(\epsilon z)a_{2}(\epsilon s,\epsilon z)\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{1}{\beta}(x+f)f^{\prime\prime}\phi_{2,x}.

Furthermore, we denote

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{5}}{\beta^{3}}\mathcal{F}_{3}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]\,:=\,B_{6}(\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2})-\Bigg[-\epsilon^{5}\kappa(\epsilon z)a_{2}(\epsilon s,\epsilon z)\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{1}{\beta}(x+f)f^{\prime\prime}\phi_{2,x}\Bigg].

(5.44)

Here the terms in
\frac{\epsilon^{5}}{\beta^{3}}\mathcal{F}_{3}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]
are all independent of
f^{\prime\prime},e^{\prime\prime}
and satisfies

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{5}}{\beta^{3}}\mathcal{F}_{3}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]\leq Ce^{-c|x|}\left(1+|\beta f|+\Big|\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\Big|\right).

(5.45)

6.
The gluing procedure to derive the projected form of equation (
2.35
)

6.1.
The gluing procedure

The gluing method from
[
21
]
will be used to transform problem (
2.35
) in
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}

into a projected version on the infinite strip
{\mathfrak{S}}
, in which we can use the local coordinates
(x,\tau)
in (
3.24
).
We define a smooth cut-off function

\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}(s)\,=\,\eta_{3\delta}(\epsilon|s|),

where
\eta_{\delta}(t)
is given by

\eta_{\delta}(t)\,=\,1\ \mbox{ for }\ 0\leq t\leq\delta\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\eta_{\delta}(t)\,=\,0\ \mbox{ for }\ t>2\delta,

for any fixed
\delta\leq\frac{\delta_{0}}{100}
with small
\delta_{0}=M_{0}
given in (
2.4
).
For problem (
2.35
), the global approximation
\mathbf{U}(y)
on the whole space
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
can be defined simply in the form

\mathbf{U}(y)\,=\,\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}(s)\beta(\epsilon z)w_{2}(x,\tau),\quad\forall\,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},

(6.1)

where the local approximate solution
w_{2}(x,\tau)
is defined in (
5.40
) and the relations between
y
,
(s,z)
and
(x,\tau)

are given in (
2.34
), (
2.4
), (
3.4
), (
3.13
), (
3.20
), and (
3.24
).

For a perturbation term
\Phi(y)\,=\,\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}(\epsilon|s|){\tilde{\phi}}(y)\,+\,{\tilde{\psi}}(y)
, the function
v(y)\,=\,\mathbf{U}(y)+\Phi(y)

satisfies (
2.35
) if the pair
({{\tilde{\phi}}},{{\tilde{\psi}}})
satisfies the following coupled system:

\displaystyle\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}\mathbf{L}(\tilde{\phi})\,=\,\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon}\Big[-{\mathcal{E}}+\mathbf{N}\big(\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}{\tilde{\phi}}+{\tilde{\psi}}\big)-3\,\mathbf{U}^{2}\,{\tilde{\psi}}\Big],

(6.2)

\displaystyle\Delta\tilde{\psi}-[1+\epsilon^{2}W]\tilde{\psi}+(1-\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon})3\mathbf{U}^{2}\,{\tilde{\psi}}

\displaystyle\,=\,-\epsilon^{2}(\Delta\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}){\tilde{\phi}}-2\epsilon\nabla\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}\cdot\nabla{\tilde{\phi}}-(1-\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon}){\mathcal{E}}

\displaystyle\quad+(1-\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon})\mathbf{N}\big(\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}{\tilde{\phi}}+{\tilde{\psi}}\big),

(6.3)

with

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}({\tilde{\phi}})\,=\,\Delta{\tilde{\phi}}-[1+\epsilon^{2}W]{\tilde{\phi}}+3{\bf U}^{2}{\tilde{\phi}},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}\,=\,\Delta{\bf U}-[1+\epsilon^{2}W]{\bf U}+{\bf U}^{3},

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{N}\big(\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}{\tilde{\phi}}+{\tilde{\psi}}\big)

\displaystyle\,=\,-\Big[{\bf U}+\big(\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}{\tilde{\phi}}+{\tilde{\psi}}\big)\Big]^{3}+{\bf U}^{3}+3{\bf U}^{2}\big(\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}{\tilde{\phi}}+{\tilde{\psi}}\big)

\displaystyle=-3{\bf U}\big(\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}{\tilde{\phi}}+{\tilde{\psi}}\big)^{2}-\big(\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}{\tilde{\phi}}+{\tilde{\psi}}\big)^{3}.

The term
{{\mathcal{E}}}
can be locally recast in
(x,\tau)
coordinate system by the relation

{{\mathcal{E}}}\,=\,\beta^{3}\,S(w_{2}),\quad\text{for $|\epsilon s|\leq 10\delta$},

(6.4)

where for the explicit expression of
S(w_{2})
, the reader can refer to (
5.3
).

First, given a small
\tilde{\phi}
satisfying the following decay property

|\nabla{\tilde{\phi}}|\,+\,|{\tilde{\phi}}|\,\leq\,e^{-{\upsilon_{1}\,\delta}/{\epsilon}},\,\,\,\,\mbox{for}\,\,\,|s|>\delta/\epsilon,

(6.5)

where
\upsilon_{1}
is a very small positive constant, we will solve (
6.1
).
Note that the nonlinear operator
\mathbf{N}
has a power-like behaviour with power greater than one, and a direct application of contraction mapping principle yields
that (
6.1
) has a unique (small) solution
{\tilde{\psi}}\,=\,{\tilde{\psi}}({\tilde{\phi}})
with

\|{\tilde{\psi}}({\tilde{\phi}})\|_{L^{\infty}}\,\leq\,C\,\epsilon\left[\|{\tilde{\phi}}\|_{L^{\infty}(|s|>\frac{\delta}{\epsilon})}\,+\,\|\nabla{\tilde{\phi}}\|_{L^{\infty}(|s|>\frac{\delta}{\epsilon})}\right]\,+\,e^{-\frac{\upsilon_{2}\delta}{\epsilon}},

(6.6)

where
|s|>\delta/\epsilon
denotes the complement of
\delta/\epsilon
-neighbourhood of

\Gamma_{\epsilon}
and
\upsilon_{2}
denotes a small positive constant.
Moreover, the nonlinear operator
\tilde{\psi}
satisfies a Lipschitz condition of the form

\|{\tilde{\psi}}({\tilde{\phi}}_{1})-{\tilde{\psi}}({\tilde{\phi}}_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon\left[\|{\tilde{\phi}}_{1}-{\tilde{\phi}}_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(|s|>\delta/\epsilon)}+\|\nabla{\tilde{\phi}}_{1}-\nabla{\tilde{\phi}}_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(|s|>\delta/\epsilon)}\right].

(6.7)

Therefore, after solving (
6.1
),
we can concern (
6.2
) as a local nonlinear problem involving
{\tilde{\psi}}\,=\,{\tilde{\psi}}({\tilde{\phi}})
,
which can be expressed in local coordinates
(x,\tau)
.
Using Fermi coordinates (
2.4
), (
2.34
) and the calculations in Section
3
, especially (
3.4
), (
3.13
), (
3.20
) and (
3.24
), we give the following lemma.

Lemma 6.1
.

By the relation

\displaystyle{\tilde{\phi}}(y)=\beta(\epsilon z)\phi^{*}(x,\tau),\quad\text{for $|\epsilon s|\leq 10\delta$},

(6.8)

we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\left(\phi^{*}\right)\,=\,\frac{1}{\beta^{3}}\mathbf{L}({\tilde{\phi}}),\quad\text{for $|\epsilon s|\leq 10\delta$},

(6.9)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\phi^{*})\,=\,L_{2}(\phi^{*})\ +\ \chi(\epsilon|s|)\big[\,B_{4}(\phi^{*})+B_{5}(\phi^{*})+B_{6}(\phi^{*})\,\big],

(6.10)

with

\displaystyle L_{2}(\phi^{*})=\frac{\epsilon^{2}4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\phi^{*}_{\tau\tau}+\phi^{*}_{xx}-\phi^{*}+3w_{2}^{2}\phi^{*}.

(6.11)

In the above, the smooth cut-off function
\chi(r)
is given by

\chi(t)\,=\,1,\quad\text{if}~t<10\delta,\qquad\chi(t)\,=\,0,\quad\text{if}\ t>20\delta,

and the operators
B_{4}
,
B_{5}
and
B_{6}
have been given in (
3.26
)-(
3.28
).
On the other hand, for the local form of the nonlinear part, we denote by the notation

\displaystyle{\mathcal{N}}(\phi^{*})\,=\,\frac{1}{\beta^{3}}\left[\,\mathbf{N}\big(\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}{\tilde{\phi}}+{\tilde{\psi}}\big)-3\,\mathbf{U}^{2}\,{\tilde{\psi}}\,\right],\quad\text{for $|\epsilon s|\leq 10\delta$}.

(6.12)

where
{\tilde{\psi}}
is concerned as an operator of
\tilde{\phi}
.∎

Note that the approximate solution
\mathbf{U}
has unknown parameter pair
(f,e)
, see (
6.1
).
We will deal with the following projected problem:
for given
(f,e)
with constraints in (
3.43
)-(
3.44
), finding
functions
\phi^{*}\in H^{2}({\mathfrak{S}})
,   and
c(\tau),d(\tau)\in L^{2}(0,2\pi)
,
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\phi^{*})\,=\,\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon}(s)\Big[-\frac{1}{\beta^{3}}{\mathcal{E}}+{\mathcal{N}}(\phi^{*})\Big]+c(\tau)\chi(\epsilon|s|)U^{\prime}(x)+d(\tau)\chi(\epsilon|s|){\mathcal{Z}}(x),\quad\text{for}\,(x,\tau)\in\mathfrak{S},

(6.13)

\phi^{*}(x,0)\,=\,\phi^{*}(x,2\pi),\qquad\phi^{*}_{\tau}(x,0)\,=\,\phi^{*}_{\tau}(x,2\pi),\quad-\infty<x<+\infty,

(6.14)

\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\phi^{*}(x,\tau){\mathcal{Z}}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,=\,\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\phi^{*}(x,\tau)U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,=\,0,\quad 0<\tau<2\pi,

(6.15)

where the definition of
\mathfrak{S}
has been given in (
3.42
).

6.2.
Solvability of the linear problem

We shall mention that we here basically follow the method in
[
21
]
to show the resolution theory for the linear problem.

We first consider the linear problem

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{2}4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\phi_{\tau\tau}+\phi_{xx}-\phi+3U^{2}\phi=h,\quad\text{for}\,(x,\tau)\in\mathfrak{S},

(6.16)

\displaystyle\phi(x,0)=\phi(x,2\pi),\quad\phi_{\tau}(x,0)=\phi_{\tau}(x,2\pi),\quad\text{for}~x\in{\mathbb{R}},

(6.17)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(x,\tau)\,\mathcal{Z}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(x,\tau)\,U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,=\,0,\quad\text{for}~\tau\in[0,2\pi),

(6.18)

and then give the following lemma.

Lemma 6.2
.

Suppose
\phi
solves (
6.16
)-(
6.18
). Then there exists a constant
C>0
, independent of
\epsilon
, such that

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})},

(6.19)

where the norm
\|\cdot\|_{{}_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}}
is given by

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}=\left({\displaystyle\int}_{\mathfrak{S}}\Bigg[\,|\phi|^{2}+|\phi_{x}|^{2}+|\phi_{xx}|^{2}+\Big|\frac{\epsilon}{\tilde{\ell}}\phi_{x\tau}\Big|^{2}+\Big|\frac{\epsilon}{\tilde{\ell}}\phi_{\tau}\Big|^{2}+\Big|\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}\phi_{\tau\tau}\Big|^{2}\,\Bigg]\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(6.20)

with
{\tilde{\ell}}
given in (
2.24
).
Moreover,
we have

\displaystyle\|\beta\phi\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\|\beta h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}.

(6.21)

Proof.

We express
h
and
\phi
as

\displaystyle h(x,\tau)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big[h_{1k}(x)\cos k\tau+h_{2k}(x)\sin k\tau\Big],

(6.22)

\displaystyle\phi(x,\tau)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big[\phi_{1k}(x)\cos k\tau+\phi_{2k}(x)\sin k\tau\Big].

(6.23)

Then we can get

\displaystyle-\frac{\epsilon^{2}k^{2}4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\phi_{jk}+\phi_{jk,xx}-\phi_{jk}+3U^{2}\phi_{jk}=h_{jk}\quad x\in{\mathbb{R}},

(6.24)

and
\phi_{jk}
satisfies the orthogonality conditions

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{jk}{\mathcal{Z}}\,{\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{jk}U^{\prime}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,=\,0,

(6.25)

where
j=1,2
and
k=0,1,2,\cdots
.

We first derive the estimates for
\phi_{jk}
with
j=1,2
and
k=0,1,2,\cdots
.

\clubsuit
Consider the bilinear form

\displaystyle B(\psi,\psi)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big[|\psi_{x}|^{2}+(1-3U^{2})|\psi|^{2}\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x.

Since (
6.25
), we can get

\displaystyle\|\phi_{jk}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}+\|\phi_{jk,x}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq CB(\phi_{jk},\phi_{jk}).

(6.26)

\clubsuit
Using (
6.24
) and (
6.26
), we can claim that

\displaystyle\Bigg[1+\frac{\epsilon^{4}k^{4}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{4}}\Bigg]\|\phi_{jk}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}+\Bigg[1+\frac{\epsilon^{2}k^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\Bigg]\|\phi_{jk,x}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq C\|h_{jk}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}.

(6.27)

In fact, from (
6.24
), we have

\displaystyle\Bigg[1+\frac{\epsilon^{2}k^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\Bigg]\|\phi_{jk}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}+\|\phi_{jk,x}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq C\|h_{jk}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}.

If
\frac{\epsilon^{2}k^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\leq 1
, then we have
\frac{\epsilon^{4}k^{4}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{4}}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{2}k^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}
. We can obtain (
6.27
) directly .

If
\frac{\epsilon^{2}k^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\geq 1
, then from (
6.24
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{2}k^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}B(\phi_{jk},\phi_{jk})+\frac{\epsilon^{4}k^{4}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{4}}\|\phi_{jk}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq C\|h_{jk}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}+(1-c)\frac{\epsilon^{4}k^{4}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{4}}\|\phi_{jk}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})},

i.e.,

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{2}k^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}B(\phi_{jk},\phi_{jk})+c\frac{\epsilon^{4}k^{4}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{4}}\|\phi_{jk}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq C\|h_{jk}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})},

with
c\in(0,1)
.
Then by (
6.26
), we get (
6.27
).

\clubsuit
By (
6.27
) and (
6.24
), we can directly get

\displaystyle\|\phi_{jk,xx}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq C\|h_{jk}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}.

(6.28)

From the expression of
\phi
as in (
6.23
), we have

\displaystyle\phi_{x}(x,\tau)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big[\phi_{1k,x}(x)\cos k\tau+\phi_{2k,x}(x)\sin k\tau\Big],

\displaystyle\phi_{xx}(x,\tau)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big[\phi_{1k,xx}(x)\cos k\tau+\phi_{2k,xx}(x)\sin k\tau\Big],

\displaystyle\phi_{\tau}(x,\tau)

\displaystyle=k\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big[-\phi_{1k}(x)\sin k\tau+\phi_{2k}(x)\cos k\tau\Big],

\displaystyle\phi_{\tau\tau}(x,\tau)

\displaystyle=-k^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big[\phi_{1k}(x)\cos k\tau+\phi_{2k}(x)\sin k\tau\Big],

\displaystyle\phi_{x\tau}(x,\tau)

\displaystyle=k\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big[-\phi_{1k,x}(x)\sin k\tau+\phi_{2k,x}(x)\cos k\tau\Big].

Therefore, we can derive that

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\pi\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left[\|\phi_{1k}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}+\|\phi_{2k}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\right],

\displaystyle\|\phi_{x}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\pi\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left[\|\phi_{1k,x}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}+\|\phi_{2k,x}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\right],

\displaystyle\|\phi_{xx}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\pi\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left[\|\phi_{1k,xx}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}+\|\phi_{2k,xx}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\right],

\displaystyle\|\phi_{\tau}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\pi\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left[k^{2}\|\phi_{1k}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}+k^{2}\|\phi_{2k}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\right],

\displaystyle\|\phi_{\tau\tau}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\pi\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left[k^{4}\|\phi_{1k}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}+k^{4}\|\phi_{2k}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\right],

\displaystyle\|\phi_{x\tau}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\pi\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\left[k^{2}\|\phi_{1k,x}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}+k^{2}\|\phi_{2k,x}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\right].

Then from (
6.27
), (
6.28
), we have

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}+\|\phi_{x}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}+\|\phi_{xx}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}\|\phi_{\tau}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\tilde{\ell}^{4}}\|\phi_{\tau\tau}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}\|\phi_{x\tau}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|h_{jk}\|^{2}_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}})}\leq C\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}.

This finishes the proof of (
6.19
).

Next, we consider the proof of (
6.21
).
By denoting

\hat{\phi}=\beta\phi,

we have,
for
\,(x,\tau)\in\mathfrak{S}
,

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{2}4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\Big(\frac{\hat{\phi}}{\beta}\Big)_{\tau\tau}+\frac{1}{\beta}\Big[\hat{\phi}_{xx}-\hat{\phi}+3U^{2}\hat{\phi}\Big]=h,

i.e.,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\beta}\Big[\frac{\epsilon^{2}4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\hat{\phi}_{\tau\tau}+\hat{\phi}_{xx}-\hat{\phi}+3U^{2}\hat{\phi}\Big]+\frac{\epsilon^{2}4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\Big[2\hat{\phi}_{\tau}\Big(\frac{1}{\beta}\Big)_{\tau}+\hat{\phi}\Big(\frac{1}{\beta}\Big)_{\tau\tau}\Big]=h,

i.e.,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\beta}\Big[\frac{\epsilon^{2}4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\hat{\phi}_{\tau\tau}+\hat{\phi}_{xx}-\hat{\phi}+3U^{2}\hat{\phi}\Big]+\frac{\epsilon^{2}4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\Big[2\hat{\phi}_{\tau}\Big(-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta^{2}}\frac{\tilde{\ell}}{2\pi\beta}\Big)+\hat{\phi}\Big(-\frac{\beta^{\prime\prime}}{\beta^{3}}+3\frac{|\beta^{\prime}|^{2}}{\beta^{4}}\Big)\frac{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}\beta}\Big]=h,

with

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{1}{\beta}\Big)_{\tau}=-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta^{2}}\frac{\tilde{\ell}}{2\pi\beta},\qquad\Big(\frac{1}{\beta}\Big)_{\tau\tau}=\Big(-\frac{\beta^{\prime\prime}}{\beta^{3}}+3\frac{|\beta^{\prime}|^{2}}{\beta^{4}}\Big)\frac{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}\beta}.

Then, by setting

\displaystyle\tilde{h}

\displaystyle=\beta h-\epsilon^{2}\Big[2\hat{\phi}_{\tau}\Big(-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta^{2}}\frac{2\pi}{\tilde{\ell}}\Big)+\hat{\phi}\Big(-\frac{\beta^{\prime\prime}}{\beta^{3}}+3\frac{|\beta^{\prime}|^{2}}{\beta^{4}}\Big)\Big],

we get

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{2}4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\hat{\phi}_{\tau\tau}+\hat{\phi}_{xx}-\hat{\phi}+3U^{2}\hat{\phi}=\tilde{h},

with the same conditions in (
6.17
)-(
6.18
) by replacing
\phi
with
\hat{\phi}
.
By (
3.17
), we get (
6.21
) directly from (
6.19
).
We finish the proof.
∎

Next, we consider the following projected problem

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{2}4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\phi_{\tau\tau}+\phi_{xx}-\phi+3U^{2}\phi=h+c(\tau)U^{\prime}(x)+d(\tau){\mathcal{Z}}(x),\quad\text{for}\,(x,\tau)\in\mathfrak{S},

(6.29)

\displaystyle\phi(x,0)=\phi(x,2\pi),\quad\phi_{\tau}(x,0)=\phi_{\tau}(x,2\pi),\quad\text{for}~x\in{\mathbb{R}},

(6.30)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(x,\tau){\mathcal{Z}}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi(x,\tau)U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,=\,0,\quad\text{for}~\tau\in[0,2\pi).

(6.31)

Lemma 6.3
.

Problem (
6.29
)-(
6.31
) has a unique solution with the estimates

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})},

(6.32)

and

\displaystyle\|\beta\phi\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\|\beta h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}.

(6.33)

Proof.

Similar to the analysis in Lemma
6.2
, by the expansions (
6.22
)-(
6.23
), we transfer (
6.29
)-(
6.31
) to the following

\displaystyle-\frac{\epsilon^{2}k^{2}4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\phi_{jk}+\phi_{jk,xx}-\phi_{jk}+3U^{2}\phi_{jk}=h_{jk}+c_{jk}U^{\prime}+d_{jk}\mathcal{Z}\quad\mbox{in }{\mathbb{R}},

(6.34)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{jk}{\mathcal{Z}}\,{\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{jk}U^{\prime}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,=\,0,

(6.35)

where
j=1,2
and
k=0,1,2,\cdots
.
By Fredholm’s theory, we know that problem (
6.34
)-(
6.35
) is solvable if

\displaystyle c_{jk}=-\frac{\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}h_{jk}U^{\prime}\,{\mathrm{d}}x}{\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}|U^{\prime}|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x},\qquad d_{jk}=-\frac{\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}h_{jk}{\mathcal{Z}}\,{\mathrm{d}}x}{\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\mathcal{Z}}^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x},

where
j=1,2
and
k=0,1,2,\cdots
.
Then define

\displaystyle\phi(x,\tau)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big[\phi_{1k}(x)\cos k\tau+\phi_{2k}(x)\sin k\tau\Big],

and correspondingly

\displaystyle c(\tau)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big[c_{1k}\cos k\tau+c_{2k}\sin k\tau\Big],

\displaystyle d(\tau)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big[d_{1k}\cos k\tau+d_{2k}\sin k\tau\Big].

By Lemma
6.2
, we have

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\ \Big(\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+\|c(\tau)U^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+\|d(\tau){\mathcal{Z}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\Big).

(6.36)

There holds

\displaystyle\|c(\tau)U^{\prime}(x)\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big(c_{1k}^{2}+c_{2k}^{2}\Big)\leq C\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|h_{jk}|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\leq C\|h\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}.

(6.37)

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\|d(\tau){\mathcal{Z}}(x)\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}^{2}\leq C\|h\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}.

(6.38)

By (
6.36
), (
6.37
), (
6.38
), we get (
6.32
).

The proof of (
6.33
) is similar to (
6.21
), and we omit it for concise writing.
∎

Consider the projection problem

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\phi^{*})=h+c(\tau)U^{\prime}(x)+d(\tau){\mathcal{Z}}(x),\quad\text{for}\,(x,\tau)\in\mathfrak{S},

(6.39)

\displaystyle\phi^{*}(x,0)=\phi^{*}(x,2\pi),\qquad\phi^{*}_{\tau}(x,0)=\phi^{*}_{\tau}(x,2\pi),\quad\text{for}~x\in{\mathbb{R}},

(6.40)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi^{*}(x,\tau){\mathcal{Z}}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi^{*}(x,\tau)U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,=\,0,\quad\text{for}~\tau\in[0,2\pi).

(6.41)

The operator
\mathcal{L}
is given in (
6.10
).
The invertibility of
\mathcal{L}
in the projection space can be easily obtained from Lemma
6.3

and the Contraction Mapping Theorem.

Lemma 6.4
.

If assumptions in
(A1)
and
(A2)
hold, then problem (
6.39
)-(
6.41
) has a unique solution with the estimates

\displaystyle\|\phi^{*}\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\|h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})},

(6.42)

and

\displaystyle\|\beta\phi\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq C\|\beta h\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}.

(6.43)

for a universal constant
C
.

Proof.

By Lemma
6.3
, combined with the Contraction Mapping Theorem, the proof can be completed.
∎

6.3.
The
L^{2}
-estimates of the error terms

We denote

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{\delta}=\mathfrak{S}\cap\Big\{|s|<\frac{10\delta}{\epsilon}\Big\}.

Note that, if
|s|\leq{10\delta}/\epsilon
, there holds

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\beta^{3}}{{\mathcal{E}}}\,=\,S(w_{2}).

From the expression of
S(w_{2})
as in (
5.3
), we can decompose
S(w_{2})
as

\displaystyle S(w_{2})=E_{11}+E_{12},

(6.44)

where

\displaystyle E_{12}=\epsilon^{3}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}e^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{Z}+\epsilon\lambda_{0}e{\mathcal{Z}},

(6.45)

and

\displaystyle\beta E_{11}=

\displaystyle\beta\Big\{\epsilon^{2}S_{3}+\epsilon^{2}S_{42}+\epsilon^{3}S_{5}+\epsilon^{3}S_{6}+\epsilon^{3}S_{7}+\epsilon^{3}S_{8}+\epsilon^{2}{\bf N}_{02}+\epsilon^{3}{\bf N}_{03}+\epsilon^{2}{\bf B}_{42}\Big\}

\displaystyle+\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{\kappa a_{2}}{\,\beta\,}e^{\prime\prime}(x+f)\mathcal{Z}-\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{\kappa a_{2}}{\,\beta\,}(x+f)f^{\prime\prime}e\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta}\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle+\beta\Bigg\{\epsilon^{3}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}{\phi}_{1,\tau\tau}+\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\phi_{2,\tau\tau}+B_{5}(\epsilon\phi_{1})+B_{6}(\epsilon\phi_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{G}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}},e,\frac{e^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\mathbb{N}_{1}(\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2})+B_{4}(\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2})+B_{5}(\epsilon^{2}{\phi_{2}})+B_{6}(\epsilon^{2}{\phi_{2}})\Bigg\}.

(6.46)

We give the following lemma.

Lemma 6.5
.

By the assumption
\bf{(B3)}
, the expression of
S(w_{2})
in (
5.3
) and decomposition (
6.44
), we easily get

\displaystyle\|\beta E_{11}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}\leq C\left(\epsilon^{3}+\epsilon^{2}\|f\|_{*}+\epsilon^{2}\|e\|_{**}\right)\leq C\left(\epsilon^{3}+\epsilon^{3-\alpha}+\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}\varrho+\frac{5}{2}-2\alpha}\right)\leq C\epsilon^{3-\alpha},

(6.47)

and

\displaystyle\big\|\beta\left(E_{11}(f_{1},e_{1})-E_{11}(f_{2},e_{2})\right)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}\leq C\epsilon^{2}\Big[\|f_{1}-f_{2}\|_{*}+\|e_{1}-e_{2}\|_{**}\Big],

(6.48)

for any
(f_{1},e_{1})
and
(f_{2},e_{2})
satisfy (
3.43
)-(
3.44
).

Proof.

Here we only give the proof of (
6.47
).
The proof of (
6.48
) is similar to (
6.47
) and we omit it for concise.
We note that
(f,e)
satisfies the constraints (
3.43
)-(
3.44
). Moreover, recall (
3.45
), we have

\displaystyle\|\beta f(\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,2\pi)}\leq C\tilde{\ell}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{*}\leq\epsilon^{\frac{1+\varrho-2\alpha}{2}}\ll 1,\qquad\Big\|\frac{\beta f^{\prime}(\tau)}{\tilde{\ell}}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(0,2\pi)}\leq C\tilde{\ell}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|f\|_{*}\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{1+\varrho-2\alpha}{2}}\ll 1,

(6.49)

and

\displaystyle\|e\|_{L^{\infty}(0,2\pi)}\leq\|e\|_{**}\leq C\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}\varrho+\frac{1}{2}-2\alpha}.

(6.50)

Next, we will give the estimates of
\|\beta E_{11}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}
.

\clubsuit

Noting the expressions of
\epsilon^{2}S_{3},\epsilon^{2}S_{42},\epsilon^{3}S_{5},\epsilon^{3}S_{6},\epsilon^{3}S_{7},\epsilon^{3}S_{8}
are given in (
5.1.2
), (
5.2.2
), (
5.1.2
), (
5.1.2
), (
5.1.2
), (
5.1.2
), then we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{|s|\leq\frac{10\delta}{\epsilon}}\Big|\beta\big(\epsilon^{2}S_{3}+\epsilon^{2}S_{42}+\epsilon^{3}S_{5}+\epsilon^{3}S_{6}+\epsilon^{3}S_{7}+\epsilon^{3}S_{8}\big)\Big|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\Big[\epsilon^{4}|\beta\,S_{3}|^{2}+\epsilon^{4}|\beta\,S_{42}|^{2}+\epsilon^{6}|\beta\,S_{5}|^{2}+\epsilon^{6}|\beta\,S_{6}|^{2}+\epsilon^{6}|\beta\,S_{7}|^{2}+\epsilon^{6}|\beta\,S_{8}|^{2}\Big]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\epsilon^{6}+\epsilon^{4}\|f\|_{*}^{2}+\epsilon^{4}\|e\|_{**}^{2}\big).

(6.51)

\clubsuit

Next, by (
5.35
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{|s|\leq\frac{10\delta}{\epsilon}}\epsilon^{4}\beta^{2}|{\bf N}_{02}|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\epsilon^{4}\|e\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,2\pi)}+\epsilon^{4}\|\beta fe\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,2\pi)}+\epsilon^{4}\|(\beta f)^{2}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,2\pi)}+\epsilon^{4}\|\beta f\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,2\pi)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\epsilon^{4}\|f\|_{*}^{2}+\epsilon^{4}\|e\|_{**}^{2}\big).

(6.52)

The last inequality holds due to (
6.49
)-(
6.50
).

\clubsuit

By (
5.28
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{|s|\leq\frac{10\delta}{\epsilon}}\epsilon^{4}\beta^{2}|{\bf B}_{42}|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\epsilon^{4}\|(\beta f)^{2}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,2\pi)}+\epsilon^{4}\|\beta f\|^{2}_{L^{2}(0,2\pi)}\right)\leq C\left(\epsilon^{4}\|f\|_{*}^{2}+\epsilon^{4}\|e\|_{**}^{2}\right).

(6.53)

\clubsuit

By the expression of
a_{2}(\epsilon s,\epsilon z)
given in (
5.1.1
), we know that

|a_{2}(\epsilon s,\epsilon z)|\leq 2,\quad\text{if}~|\epsilon s|\leq 10\delta.

Then we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{|s|\leq\frac{10\delta}{\epsilon}}\beta^{2}\left|\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}a_{2}\frac{\kappa}{\,\beta\,}e^{\prime\prime}(x+f)\mathcal{Z}\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{|s|\leq\frac{10\delta}{\epsilon}}\beta^{2}\left|\epsilon^{4}\kappa a_{2}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{1}{\,\beta\,}(x+f)f^{\prime\prime}e\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\epsilon^{4}\|f\|_{*}^{2}+\epsilon^{4}\|e\|_{**}^{2}\right).

(6.54)

\clubsuit

By the expression of
\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}\right]
given in (
5.5
), we directly get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{|s|\leq\frac{10\delta}{\epsilon}}\beta^{2}\left|\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta}\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}\right]\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}\tau\leq C\left(\epsilon^{6}+\epsilon^{4}\|f\|_{*}^{2}\right).

(6.55)

\clubsuit

By (
5.2.1
), we have

\displaystyle\epsilon^{6}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{|s|\leq\frac{10\delta}{\epsilon}}\beta^{2}\Big|\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}{\phi}_{1,\tau\tau}\Big|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\epsilon^{6}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{|s|\leq\frac{10\delta}{\epsilon}}\left[\beta^{2}|{\mathbf{b}}_{3}|^{2}\left(\varpi_{1}^{2}+f^{2}\varpi_{2}^{2}\right)+\beta^{2}|{\mathbf{b}}_{2}|^{2}\Big|\frac{f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\Big|^{2}\varpi_{2}^{2}+\beta^{2}|{\mathbf{b}}_{1}|^{2}\Big|\frac{f^{\prime\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}\Big|^{2}\varpi_{2}^{2}\right]\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\epsilon^{6}+\epsilon^{4}\|f\|_{*}^{2}\big).

(6.56)

Here we note that we used the estimates

|{\mathbf{b}}_{\ell}|\leq C\frac{1}{\beta},\quad\text{for}~\ell=1,2,3.

\clubsuit

We recall the condition (
2.1.2
) and the definition of
\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2}
given in (
5.38
). By some tedious but straight calculations, we know that

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\phi_{2,\tau\tau}\Big|\leq C\frac{1}{\beta^{2}}e^{-|x|}.

(6.57)

Then

\displaystyle\epsilon^{8}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{|s|\leq\frac{10\delta}{\epsilon}}\beta^{2}\Big|\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}{\phi}_{2,\tau\tau}\Big|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}\tau\leq C\epsilon^{8}.

(6.58)

\clubsuit

By (
5.2.2
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{|s|\leq\frac{10\delta}{\epsilon}}\beta^{2}\left|B_{5}(\epsilon\phi_{1})\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}\tau\leq C\left(\epsilon^{6}+\epsilon^{4}\|f\|_{*}^{2}+\epsilon^{4}\|e\|_{**}^{2}\right).

(6.59)

Moreover, we recall the decomposition

\displaystyle B_{6}(\epsilon\phi_{1})=\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{2}\left[\theta,x,f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}\right],

where
\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{2}\left[\theta,x,f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right],\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}\right]
are defined in (
5.2.2
), (
5.30
)
and satisfies

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{2}\left[\theta,x,f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]\right|\leq\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}e^{-c|x|}\left(1+\beta|f|+\Big|\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\Big|\right),

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}\right]\right|\leq\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}e^{-c|x|}\left(1+\beta|f|+\Big|\frac{\beta f^{\prime\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}\Big|\right),

if
|\epsilon s|\leq 10\delta
.
Then we can directly get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{|s|\leq\frac{10\delta}{\epsilon}}\beta^{2}\left|B_{6}(\epsilon\phi_{1})\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}\tau\leq C\left(\epsilon^{6}+\epsilon^{4}\|f\|_{*}^{2}\right).

(6.60)

\clubsuit

By (
5.1.1
), (
5.5
), if
|\epsilon s|\leq 10\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]\right|\leq\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}e^{-c|x|}\left(1+\beta|f|+\Big|\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\Big|\right),

\displaystyle\left|\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{G}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}},e,\frac{e^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]\right|\leq\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}e^{-c|x|}\left(1+\beta|f|+\Big|\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\Big|+|e|+\Big|\frac{e^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\Big|\right).

Then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{|s|\leq\frac{10\delta}{\epsilon}}\Bigg\{\beta^{2}\left|\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]\right|^{2}+\beta^{2}\left|\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{G}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}},e,\frac{e^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]\right|^{2}\Bigg\}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\epsilon^{6}+\epsilon^{4}\|f\|_{*}^{2}+\epsilon^{4}\|e\|_{**}^{2}\right).

(6.61)

\clubsuit

At last, by (
5.38
) and recalling the definition of
B_{4},B_{5},B_{6}
given in (
3.26
), (
3.2
), (
3.28
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{|s|\leq\frac{10\delta}{\epsilon}}\beta^{2}\left|\mathbb{N}_{1}(\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2})+B_{4}(\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2})+B_{5}(\epsilon^{2}{\phi_{2}})+B_{6}(\epsilon^{2}{\phi_{2}})\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,{\mathrm{d}}\tau

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\epsilon^{6}+\epsilon^{4}\|f\|_{*}^{2}+\epsilon^{4}\|e\|_{**}^{2}\right).

(6.62)

Combining all the estimates in (
6.3
)-(
6.3
),
we can get (
6.47
).
∎

6.4.
Solving the nonlinear intermediate problem

In this part, by using the facts given in the above, we will solve problem (
6.13
)-(
6.15
).
The term

E_{12}=\epsilon^{3}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}e^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{Z}+\epsilon\lambda_{0}e{\mathcal{Z}}

has precisely the form
d\chi{\mathcal{Z}}
and can be absorbed in the term.
Therefore, problem (
6.13
)-(
6.15
) can be replaced by the following

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\phi^{*})\,=\,\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon}(s)\Big[-E_{11}+{\mathcal{N}}(\phi^{*})\Big]+c(\tau)\chi(\epsilon|x|)U^{\prime}(x)+d(\tau)\chi(\epsilon|x|){\mathcal{Z}}(x),\quad\text{for}\,(x,\tau)\in\mathfrak{S},

(6.63)

{\phi^{*}}(x,0)\,=\,{\phi^{*}}(x,2\pi),\quad\phi^{*}_{\tau}(x,0)\,=\,\phi^{*}_{\tau}(x,2\pi),\quad-\infty<x<+\infty,

(6.64)

\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\phi^{*}(x,\tau){\mathcal{Z}}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,=\,\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}\phi^{*}(x,\tau)U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,=\,0,\quad 0<\tau<2\pi.

(6.65)

Let
{\mathcal{T}}
be the operator defined by Lemma
6.4
,
then problem (
6.63
)-(
6.65
) is equivalent to the fixed-point problem

\displaystyle\phi^{*}={\mathcal{T}}\big(-E_{11}+{\mathcal{N}}(\phi^{*})\big):=\mathbb{A}(\phi^{*}).

(6.66)

By the effect of cut-off function
\chi(\epsilon|x|)
, using the maximum principle and Sobolev embedding, we can get

\displaystyle|\left(\beta\phi^{*}\right)(x,\tau)|\,+\,|\nabla\left(\beta\phi^{*}\right)(x,\tau)|\,\leq\,\|\beta\phi^{*}\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}e^{-\frac{2\delta}{\epsilon}},\quad\text{for}~|s|\geq\frac{40\delta}{\varepsilon},

where the norm
\|\cdot\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}
is defined in (
6.20
).
In fact, from the expression of
\mathcal{L}(\phi^{*})
given in (
6.10
), we know that, if
|\epsilon s|>20\delta
,
\phi^{*}
satisfies an equation of the form

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{2}4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\phi^{*}_{\tau\tau}+\phi^{*}_{xx}-\big(1+o(1)\big)\phi^{*}=0.

(6.67)

Then
we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\beta}\left[\frac{\epsilon^{2}4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\left(\beta\phi^{*}\right)_{\tau\tau}+2\epsilon^{2}\Big(-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta^{2}}\frac{2\pi}{\tilde{\ell}}\Big)\left(\beta\phi^{*}\right)_{\tau}+\left(\beta\phi^{*}\right)_{xx}-\big(1+o(1)\big)\left(\beta\phi^{*}\right)\right]=0.

For
|s|\geq\frac{20\delta}{\epsilon}
, we use a barrier function of the form

\displaystyle\varphi(x,\tau)=C\|\beta\phi^{*}\|_{\infty}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\big(x-\frac{20\beta\delta}{\epsilon}\big)}.

It’s easy to verify that
\varphi(x,\tau)
is a sup-solution of (
6.67
)
and satisfies

\displaystyle\varphi(x,\tau)>\left|\left(\beta\phi^{*}\right)(x,\tau)\right|,\quad\mbox{at}\ s=\frac{20\delta}{\epsilon},

where the relation between
s
and
x
is given by (
3.13
) and (
3.24
).
Then we have

\displaystyle|\left(\beta\phi^{*}\right)(x,\tau)|\leq C\|\beta\phi^{*}\|_{\infty}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\big(x-\frac{20\beta\delta}{\epsilon}\big)}\leq C\|\beta\phi^{*}\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}e^{-\frac{5\beta\delta}{\epsilon}},\quad\text{for}~|s|\geq\frac{40\delta}{\varepsilon}.

The estimate of
\nabla\phi^{*}
follows by local elliptic estimate and we omit the proof for brevity.

By defining the following closed and bounded set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}=

\displaystyle\Bigg\{\phi(x,\tau)\,:\,\phi~\text{satisfies}~\eqref{periodic condition},\qquad\|\beta\phi\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq D\left(\epsilon^{3}+\epsilon^{2}\|f\|_{*}+\epsilon^{2}\|e\|_{**}\right),

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\Big\|\,|\left(\beta\phi\right)(x,\tau)|+|\nabla\left(\beta\phi\right)(x,\tau)|\,\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(|s|\geq\frac{40\delta}{\varepsilon})}\leq\|\beta\phi\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}e^{-\frac{2\delta}{\epsilon}}\Bigg\},

we claim that:

the map
\mathbb{A}
defined in (
6.66
) is a contraction map from
\mathfrak{B}
to itself.

In fact, recall

\displaystyle{\mathcal{N}}(\phi^{*})

\displaystyle\,=\,\frac{1}{\beta^{3}}\left[\mathbf{N}(\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}{\tilde{\phi}}+{\tilde{\psi}})-3\,\mathbf{U}^{2}\,{\tilde{\psi}}(\tilde{\phi})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\beta^{3}}\left[-3\mathbf{U}\left(\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}{\tilde{\phi}}+{\tilde{\psi}}\right)^{2}-\left(\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}{\tilde{\phi}}+{\tilde{\psi}}\right)^{3}-3\,\mathbf{U}^{2}\,{\tilde{\psi}}(\tilde{\phi})\right],

with
\mathbf{U}(y)
and
\tilde{\phi}(y)
as in (
6.1
) and (
6.8
).

For any
\phi_{1}^{*},\phi_{2}^{*}\in\mathfrak{B}
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|{\mathcal{N}}(\phi_{1}^{*})-{\mathcal{N}}(\phi_{2}^{*})\Big|

\displaystyle=\frac{C}{\beta^{3}}\Bigg|-3\mathbf{U}\left(\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}{\tilde{\phi}}_{1}+{\tilde{\psi}}_{1}\right)^{2}+3\mathbf{U}\left(\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}{\tilde{\phi}}_{2}+{\tilde{\psi}}_{2}\right)^{2}-\left(\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}{\tilde{\phi}}_{1}+{\tilde{\psi}}_{1}\right)^{3}+\left(\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}{\tilde{\phi}}_{2}+{\tilde{\psi}}_{2}\right)^{3}-3\,\mathbf{U}^{2}(\tilde{\psi}_{1}-\tilde{\psi}_{2})\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\beta^{3}}\Bigg[\,\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}\mathbf{U}\big|{\tilde{\phi}}_{1}^{2}-{\tilde{\phi}}_{2}^{2}\big|+\mathbf{U}\big|{\tilde{\psi}}_{1}^{2}-{\tilde{\psi}}_{2}^{2}\big|+\mathbf{U}\big(|\tilde{\psi}_{1}|+|\tilde{\psi}_{2}|\big)\big|{\tilde{\phi}}_{1}-{\tilde{\phi}}_{2}\big|+\mathbf{U}\big(|\tilde{\phi}_{1}|+|\tilde{\phi}_{2}|\big)\big|{\tilde{\psi}}_{1}-{\tilde{\psi}}_{2}\big|\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad\ +\ \frac{C}{\beta^{3}}\big(|\tilde{\phi}_{1}|^{2}+|\tilde{\phi}_{2}|^{2}+|\tilde{\psi}_{1}|^{2}+|\tilde{\psi}_{2}|^{2}\big)\big(|\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{2}|+|\tilde{\psi}_{1}-\tilde{\psi}_{2}|\big)\ +\ \frac{C}{\beta^{3}}\mathbf{U}^{2}|\tilde{\psi}_{1}-\tilde{\psi}_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq C|w_{2}|\big(|{\phi}_{1}^{*}|+|{\phi}_{2}^{*}|\big)\,|\phi^{*}_{1}-\phi^{*}_{2}|\ +\ \frac{C}{\beta^{2}}|w_{2}|\big(|{\tilde{\psi}}_{1}|+|{\tilde{\psi}}_{2}|\big)\,|\tilde{\psi}_{1}-\tilde{\psi}_{2}|

\displaystyle\quad\ +\ \frac{C}{\beta}\Big[\,|w_{2}|\,\big(|{\phi}_{1}^{*}|+|{\phi}_{2}^{*}|\big)+|{\phi}_{1}^{*}|^{2}+|{\phi}_{2}^{*}|^{2}\,\Big]\,|\tilde{\psi}_{1}-\tilde{\psi}_{2}|\ +\ \frac{C}{\beta}|w_{2}|\,\big(|\tilde{\psi}_{1}|+\tilde{\psi}_{2}|\big)|{\phi}_{1}^{*}-{\phi}_{2}^{*}|

\displaystyle\quad\ +\ C\big(|{\phi}_{1}^{*}|^{2}+|{\phi}_{2}^{*}|^{2}\big)\,|\phi^{*}_{1}-\phi^{*}_{2}|\ +\ \frac{C}{\beta^{2}}\big(|{\tilde{\psi}}_{1}|^{2}+|{\tilde{\psi}}_{2}|^{2}\big)\,|\phi^{*}_{1}-\phi^{*}_{2}|

\displaystyle\quad\ +\ \frac{1}{\beta^{3}}\big(|{\tilde{\psi}}_{1}|^{2}+|{\tilde{\psi}}_{2}|^{2}\big)\,|\tilde{\psi}_{1}-\tilde{\psi}_{2}|\ +\ \frac{C}{\beta}|w_{2}|^{2}|\tilde{\psi}_{1}-\tilde{\psi}_{2}|.

(6.68)

Here we have used the notation

\tilde{\psi}_{j}={\tilde{\psi}}({\tilde{\phi}}_{j}),\quad j=1,2.

By Lemma
6.4
, we can get

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{A}(\phi^{*}_{1})-\mathbb{A}(\phi^{*}_{2})\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}

\displaystyle=\|{\mathcal{T}}\left({\mathcal{N}}(\phi^{*}_{1})-{\mathcal{N}}(\phi^{*}_{2})\right)\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}\leq\|{\mathcal{T}}\|\,\|{\mathcal{N}}(\phi_{1}^{*})-{\mathcal{N}}(\phi_{2}^{*})\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})},

(6.69)

where
{\mathcal{T}}
is the linear operator given in Lemma
6.4
.
Then from (
6.4
), we can get

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{N}}(\phi_{1}^{*})-{\mathcal{N}}(\phi_{2}^{*})\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\|\phi_{1}^{*}\|_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S})}+\|\phi_{2}^{*}\|_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S})}+\Big\|\frac{{\tilde{\psi}}_{1}}{\beta}\Big\|_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}+\Big\|\frac{{\tilde{\psi}}_{2}}{\beta}\Big\|_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}\Big)\|\phi_{1}^{*}-\phi_{2}^{*}\|_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S})}

\displaystyle\quad+\Big(\|\phi_{1}^{*}\|_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S})}+\|\phi_{2}^{*}\|_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S})}+\Big\|\frac{{\tilde{\psi}}_{1}}{\beta}\Big\|_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}+\Big\|\frac{{\tilde{\psi}}_{2}}{\beta}\Big\|_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}+1\Big)\Big\|\frac{{\tilde{\psi}}_{1}-{\tilde{\psi}}_{2}}{\beta}\Big\|_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}.

(6.70)

We recall the estimate and Lipschitz property of
\tilde{\psi}

\|{\tilde{\psi}}({\tilde{\phi}})\|_{L^{\infty}}\,\leq\,C\,\epsilon\left[\|\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}(|s|>\frac{20\delta}{\epsilon})}\,+\,\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}\|_{L^{\infty}(|s|>\frac{20\delta}{\epsilon})}\right]\,+\,e^{-\frac{\upsilon_{2}\delta}{\epsilon}},

(6.71)

and

\|{\tilde{\psi}}(\tilde{\phi}_{1})-{\tilde{\psi}}(\tilde{\phi}_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\epsilon\left[\|\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\tilde{\phi}_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(|s|>{20\delta}/\epsilon)}+\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{1}-\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(|s|>{20\delta}/\epsilon)}\right].

(6.72)

Then by (
6.71
) and the relation (
6.8
), we get

\displaystyle\Big\|\frac{{\tilde{\psi}}_{1}}{\beta}\Big\|_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}+\Big\|\frac{{\tilde{\psi}}_{2}}{\beta}\Big\|_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\,\left[\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left(\|\tilde{\phi}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}(|s|>\frac{20\delta}{\epsilon})}\,+\,\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}(|s|>\frac{20\delta}{\epsilon})}\right)\right]\,+\,e^{-\frac{\upsilon_{2}\delta}{\epsilon}}\,\right\}\left(\int_{\mathfrak{S}_{\delta}}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left\{\,\left[\sum_{j=1}^{2}\left(\|\tilde{\phi}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}(|s|>\frac{20\delta}{\epsilon})}\,+\,\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}(|s|>\frac{20\delta}{\epsilon})}\right)\right]\,+\,e^{-\frac{\upsilon_{2}\delta}{\epsilon}}\,\right\}\left(\frac{\int_{0}^{2\pi}\beta{\mathrm{d}}\tau}{\epsilon}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{\int_{0}^{2\pi}\beta{\mathrm{d}}\tau}{\epsilon}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\,\sum_{j=1}^{2}\|\left(\beta\phi_{j}^{*}\right)\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}e^{-\frac{2\delta}{\epsilon}}+O(e^{-\frac{\delta}{\epsilon}})\leq Ce^{-\frac{\delta}{\epsilon}}.

(6.73)

In fact, by (
2.5
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\beta{\mathrm{d}}\tau=\frac{2\pi}{\tilde{\ell}}\int_{0}^{\ell}\beta^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\theta\leq C\epsilon^{\varrho-1}.

Therefore, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\left({\epsilon}^{-1}{\int_{0}^{2\pi}\beta{\mathrm{d}}\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}e^{-\frac{2\delta}{\epsilon}}\leq e^{-\frac{\delta}{\epsilon}},

for
\epsilon
small enough.

On the other hand, by (
6.72
), we have

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\psi}_{1}-\tilde{\psi}_{2}\|_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\tilde{\psi}_{1}-\tilde{\psi}_{2}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}\left(\int_{\mathfrak{S}_{\delta}}\,{\mathrm{d}}x{\mathrm{d}}\tau\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big[\,\|\beta\left({\phi}^{*}_{1}-{\phi}^{*}_{2}\right)\|_{L^{\infty}(|s|>{20\delta}/\epsilon)}\ +\ \|\nabla\left(\beta{\phi}^{*}_{1}-\beta{\phi}^{*}_{2}\right)\|_{L^{\infty}(|s|>{20\delta}/\epsilon)}\Big]\left(\frac{\int_{0}^{2\pi}\beta{\mathrm{d}}\tau}{\epsilon}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq o(1)\|\beta\left({\phi}^{*}_{1}-{\phi}^{*}_{2}\right)\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}.

(6.74)

The last inequality holds due to

\displaystyle\big[\,\|\beta\left({\phi}^{*}_{1}-{\phi}^{*}_{2}\right)\|_{L^{\infty}(|s|>{20\delta}/\epsilon)}+\|\nabla\left(\beta{\phi}^{*}_{1}-\beta{\phi}^{*}_{2}\right)\|_{L^{\infty}(|s|>{20\delta}/\epsilon)}\big]\leq\|\beta\left({\phi}^{*}_{1}-{\phi}^{*}_{2}\right)\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}e^{-\frac{2\delta}{\epsilon}},

for any
{\phi}^{*}_{1},{\phi}^{*}_{2}\in\mathfrak{B}.

By (
6.69
), (
6.4
), (
6.4
), (
6.4
) and Sobolev embedding, we prove that
\mathbb{A}
is a contraction map.

Next, we will show that
\mathbb{A}
maps
\mathfrak{B}
into itself.
For any
\phi^{*}\in\mathfrak{B}
, by Lemma
6.4
, then

\displaystyle\|\beta\mathbb{A}(\phi^{*})\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}=\,

\displaystyle\|\beta{\mathcal{T}}\left(-E_{11}+{\mathcal{N}}(\phi^{*})\right)\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{T}}\|\left\{\|\beta E_{11}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\ +\ \Bigg\|\frac{\beta}{\beta^{3}}\left[-3\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon}\mathbf{U}\left(\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}{\tilde{\phi}}+{\tilde{\psi}}\right)^{2}+\left(\eta_{3\delta}^{\epsilon}{\tilde{\phi}}+{\tilde{\psi}}\right)^{3}-3\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon}\,\mathbf{U}^{2}\,{\tilde{\psi}}(\tilde{\phi})\right]\Bigg\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\,

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{T}}\|\Bigg\{\|\beta E_{11}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+C\|\beta\phi^{*}\|^{2}_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S})}+C\|\tilde{\psi}\|^{2}_{L^{4}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+C\|\beta\phi^{*}\|^{3}_{L^{6}(\mathfrak{S})}+C\|\tilde{\psi}\|^{3}_{L^{6}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}+C\|\tilde{\psi}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}\Bigg\}.

(6.75)

Using (
6.71
) and similar to (
6.4
), we have

\displaystyle\|\tilde{\psi}\|_{L^{p}(\mathfrak{S}_{\delta})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\frac{\int_{0}^{2\pi}\beta{\mathrm{d}}\tau}{\epsilon}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\,\|\beta\phi^{*}\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}e^{-\frac{2\delta}{\epsilon}}+O(e^{-\frac{\delta}{\epsilon}})

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-\frac{\delta}{\epsilon}}\Big(\|\beta\phi^{*}\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}+1\Big),

(6.76)

provided with

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\int_{0}^{2\pi}\beta{\mathrm{d}}\tau}{\epsilon}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}e^{-\frac{2\delta}{\epsilon}}\leq e^{-\frac{\delta}{\epsilon}}.

By (
6.4
), (
6.4
) and Sobolev embedding, we get

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{A}(\phi^{*})\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big\{\|\beta E_{11}\|_{L^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+C\|\beta\phi^{*}\|^{2}_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}+Ce^{-\frac{\delta}{\epsilon}}\Big(\|\beta\phi^{*}\|_{H^{2}_{*}(\mathfrak{S})}+1\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\epsilon^{3}+\epsilon^{2}\|f\|_{*}+\epsilon^{2}\|e\|_{**}\right).

(6.77)

Next, for any
\phi^{*}
, since
\varphi^{*}:=\mathbb{A}(\phi^{*})
satisfies the equation

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{2}4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\varphi^{*}_{\tau\tau}+\varphi^{*}_{xx}-\big(1+o(1)\big)\varphi^{*}=h

with
h
compactly supported. Hence
\varphi^{*}
satisfies the estimates required for functions in the
\mathcal{B}
.
Then we know that
\mathbb{A}
map
\mathfrak{B}
to itself if
D
large enough.

By summarizing the above results, we present the following resolution theory.

Proposition 6.6
.

There is a number
D>0
such that for all
\epsilon
small enough and all parameters
(f,e)
in
\hat{\mathcal{F}}
,
problem (
6.13
)-(
6.15
) has a unique solution
\phi^{*}\,=\,\phi^{*}(f,e)
, which satisfies the estimates

\displaystyle\|\beta\phi^{*}\|_{H_{*}^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}+\|\phi^{*}\|_{H_{*}^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}

\displaystyle\,\leq\,D\left(\epsilon^{3}+\epsilon^{2}\|f\|_{*}+\epsilon^{2}\|e\|_{**}\right)

\displaystyle\leq D\left(\epsilon^{3}+\epsilon^{3-\alpha}+\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}\varrho+\frac{5}{2}-2\alpha}\right)\leq(D+1)\epsilon^{3-\alpha},

and

\displaystyle\big\||\beta\phi^{*}|+|\nabla\left(\beta\phi^{*}\right)|\big\|_{L^{\infty}(|x|>40\delta/\epsilon)}\,\leq\,D\|\beta\phi^{*}\|_{H_{*}^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}e^{-\delta/\epsilon}.

Moreover,
\phi^{*}
depends Lipschitz-continuously on the parameters
f
and
e
in the sense of the estimate

\|\phi^{*}(f_{1},e_{1})\,-\,\phi^{*}(f_{2},e_{2})\|_{H_{*}^{2}(\mathfrak{S})}\,\leq\,D\epsilon^{2}\big[\,\|f_{1}-f_{2}\|_{*}\,+\,\|e_{1}-e_{2}\|_{**}\,\big].

(6.78)

∎

7.
Deriving the reduced system of differential equations

After solving problem (
6.13
)-(
6.15
) in Proposition
6.6
,
we shall choose the unknown parameters
f
and
e
to make the Lagrange multipliers
c
and
d
vanish.
In fact,
c\equiv 0
and
d\equiv 0
are equivalent to

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big\{\,\mathcal{L}(\phi^{*})+\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon}(s)\frac{1}{\beta^{3}}{\mathcal{E}}-\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon}(s){\mathcal{N}}(\phi^{*})\Big\}U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,=\,0,

(7.1)

and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Big\{\,\mathcal{L}(\phi^{*})+\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon}(s)\frac{1}{\beta^{3}}{\mathcal{E}}-\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon}(s){\mathcal{N}}(\phi^{*})\Big\}{\mathcal{Z}}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\,=\,0.

(7.2)

The calculations of the above terms will be given in Sections
7.1
-
7.2
.
These will consequently derive the reduced system with differential equations involving the
parameters
f
and
e
, see Section
7.3
.

7.1.
Projections of the error terms

We will denote by
\tilde{\mathcal{D}}_{j}(\theta)
with
j\in\mathbb{N}^{+},
the smooth functions that are uniformly bounded and independent of
f,f^{\prime},f^{\prime\prime},e,e^{\prime},e^{\prime\prime}
.

7.1.1.
Computation for
\beta^{-3}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\,\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon}(s)\,{\mathcal{E}}\,U^{\prime}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

We first concern

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\beta^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon}(s){\mathcal{E}}U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\beta^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}{\mathcal{E}}U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\ +\ \frac{1}{\beta^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big[\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon}(s)-1\big]{\mathcal{E}}U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\,S\big(w_{2}\big)\,U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\ +\ \int_{\mathbb{R}}\big[\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon}(s)-1\big]S\big(w_{2}\big)U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\Bigg[\epsilon^{2}S_{3}+\epsilon^{2}S_{42}+\epsilon^{3}S_{5}+\epsilon^{3}S_{6}+\epsilon^{3}S_{7}+\epsilon^{3}S_{8}+\frac{1}{\beta}\epsilon^{3}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}e^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{Z}+\frac{1}{\beta}\epsilon\lambda_{0}e{\mathcal{Z}}+\epsilon^{2}{\bf N}_{02}+\epsilon^{2}{\bf N}_{03}+\epsilon^{2}{\bf B}_{42}

\displaystyle\qquad+\epsilon^{3}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}{\phi}_{1,\tau\tau}+\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\phi_{2,\tau\tau}+B_{5}(\epsilon\phi_{1})+\mathbb{N}_{1}(\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2})+B_{4}(\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2})+B_{5}(\epsilon^{2}{\phi_{2}})\Bigg]U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\mathfrak{G}(\theta)

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon}(s)\Bigg\{\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}a_{2}\frac{\kappa}{\,\beta^{2}\,}e^{\prime\prime}(x+f)\mathcal{Z}-\epsilon^{4}\kappa a_{2}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{1}{\,\beta^{2}\,}(x+f)f^{\prime\prime}e\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad+B_{6}(\epsilon\phi_{1})+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{G}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}},e,\frac{e^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]+B_{6}(\epsilon^{2}{\phi_{2}})\Bigg\}U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x,

due to the expression of
\mathcal{E}
given in (
6.4
),
where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big[\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon}(s)-1\big]\Bigg[\epsilon^{2}S_{3}+\epsilon^{2}S_{42}+\epsilon^{3}S_{5}+\epsilon^{3}S_{6}+\epsilon^{3}S_{7}+\epsilon^{3}S_{8}+\frac{1}{\beta}\epsilon^{3}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}e^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{Z}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{\beta}\epsilon\lambda_{0}e{\mathcal{Z}}+\epsilon^{2}{\bf N}_{02}+\epsilon^{3}{\bf N}_{03}+\epsilon^{2}{\bf B}_{42}+\epsilon^{3}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}{\phi}_{1,\tau\tau}+\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\phi_{2,\tau\tau}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+B_{5}(\epsilon\phi_{1})+\mathbb{N}_{1}(\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2})+B_{4}(\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2})+B_{5}(\epsilon^{2}{\phi_{2}})\Bigg]U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x.

Note that the terms from

\displaystyle\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}a_{2}\frac{\kappa}{\,\beta^{2}\,}e^{\prime\prime}(x+f)\mathcal{Z}-\epsilon^{4}\kappa a_{2}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{1}{\,\beta^{2}\,}(x+f)f^{\prime\prime}e\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle+B_{6}(\epsilon\phi_{1})+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{G}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}},e,\frac{e^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]+B_{6}(\epsilon^{2}{\phi_{2}})

are involving
a_{j}(t,\theta),j=1,2,3
given in (
3.11
).
By the exponential decay of the term in
S\big(w_{2}\big)
with respect to
x
,
we neglect the calculations of the terms in
\mathfrak{G}(\theta)
.

Since the following terms in
S\big(w_{2}\big)

\epsilon^{2}S_{42},\quad\epsilon^{3}S_{6},\quad\epsilon^{3}S_{8},\quad\frac{1}{\beta}\epsilon^{3}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}e^{\prime\prime}\mathcal{Z}+\frac{1}{\beta}\epsilon\lambda_{0}e{\mathcal{Z}},\quad\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\phi_{2,\tau\tau}

are even functions of
x
, then integration against
U^{\prime}(x)
just vanishes.
And there holds

\displaystyle\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{1,\tau\tau}U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x=0,

due to the fact

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\phi_{1}(x,\tau)U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x=0.

Therefore, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\beta^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon}(s){\mathcal{E}}U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\epsilon^{2}S_{3}U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\epsilon^{3}S_{5}U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\epsilon^{3}S_{7}U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\epsilon^{2}{\bf N}_{02}U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\epsilon^{3}{\bf N}_{03}U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\epsilon^{2}{\bf B}_{42}U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}B_{5}(\epsilon\phi_{1})U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\mathbb{N}_{1}(\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2})U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}B_{4}(\epsilon^{2}\phi_{2})U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}B_{5}(\epsilon^{2}{\phi_{2}})U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon}(s)\Bigg[\epsilon^{4}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}a_{2}\frac{\kappa}{\,\beta^{2}\,}e^{\prime\prime}(x+f)\mathcal{Z}-\epsilon^{4}\kappa a_{2}\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}\frac{1}{\,\beta^{2}\,}(x+f)f^{\prime\prime}e\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}\Bigg]U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon}(s)\Bigg\{\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}^{2}}\right]+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]+\frac{\epsilon^{4}}{\beta^{2}}\mathcal{G}_{1}\left[\tau,x,\beta f,\frac{\beta f^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}},e,\frac{e^{\prime}}{\tilde{\ell}}\right]\Bigg\}U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\eta_{\delta}^{\epsilon}(s)\,\Big[B_{6}(\epsilon\phi_{1})+B_{6}(\epsilon^{2}{\phi_{2}})\Big]\,U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{13}\,I_{j}.

(7.3)

We calculate these terms as follows.

\clubsuit

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\quad+\epsilon^{2}\Big[-\frac{\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}f^{\prime\prime}-\frac{6\pi}{\tilde{\ell}}\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta^{2}}f^{\prime}+\frac{2|\beta^{\prime}|^{2}}{\beta^{4}}f+\frac{\beta^{\prime\prime}}{\beta^{3}}f\,-\,\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\beta^{2}}f\Big]\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}|U^{\prime}|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{2}\varrho_{1}\Bigg\{-\frac{4\pi^{2}}{{\tilde{\ell}}^{2}}f^{\prime\prime}-\frac{4\pi}{\tilde{\ell}}\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta^{2}}f^{\prime}+\Big[\frac{|\beta^{\prime}|^{2}}{\beta^{4}}+\frac{\beta^{\prime\prime}}{\beta^{3}}\,-\,\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\beta^{2}}\Big]f+\,\frac{3}{2}\frac{\epsilon^{2}W_{tt}(0,\theta)}{\beta^{4}}f\Bigg\}+\frac{\epsilon^{2}{\mathbf{b}}_{1}d_{1}e}{\beta},

\displaystyle\quad+\Big[\frac{\kappa^{3}}{\beta^{3}}f^{2}-\frac{4\kappa|\beta^{\prime}|^{2}}{\beta^{5}}f^{2}-\frac{2\kappa\beta^{\prime\prime}}{\beta^{4}}f^{2}+\frac{12\pi}{{\tilde{\ell}}}\frac{\kappa\beta^{\prime}}{\beta^{3}}f^{\prime}f+\frac{\kappa^{\prime}\beta^{\prime}}{\beta^{4}}f^{2}-\frac{2\pi}{{\tilde{\ell}}}\frac{\kappa^{\prime}}{\beta^{2}}f^{\prime}f\Big]\int_{\mathbb{R}}|U^{\prime}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{\epsilon^{2}W_{tt}(0,\theta)}{\beta^{4}}f\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}x{\mathcal{Z}}U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x\Bigg\}e,

with

From the expression of
{\bf N}_{03}
as in (

), we have

\displaystyle I_{5}\,=\,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\epsilon^{3}{\bf N}_{03}U^{\prime}(x)\,{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\,=\,
